@oh-my-pi/pi-tui

Breaking Changes

  • MarkdownTheme.heading receives the heading depth (1–6) as a second argument; callers must pass it.
  • Shell visualization uses the native presentation engine: shell node IDs are opaque strings, and native shell nodes receive presentation data, not raw execution traces.
  • Replaced CustomEditor.modelMentionLabel with resolveModelMention (returns { label, selector }); collapseModelMentions takes the same callback; getModelMentionSuggestions returns ModelMentionCompletion.
  • SetupHost must implement autoUpdate, canAutoUpdate() and saveAutoUpdate() for setup version 3's auto-update scene.
  • Removed SPINNER_ADVANCE_MS in favor of the shared spinner clock; sharedSpinnerFrame is exported from spinner-clock.
  • Removed formatMeta from render/render-utils.
  • TranscriptContainer.nativeBlocks() and Composer.describeSurface() now require the frame's DescribeContext.
  • GradientHighlightSpec.lightness now maps gradient position to lightness like hue instead of taking a fixed percentage.

Added

  • Colorful Markdown, on by default (setMarkdownColorful): headings by level, bold/italic, inline code and table headers take distinct hues from the theme's terminal.ansi palette. MarkdownTheme gains optional strong, emphasis and tableHeader roles; Theme.ansiFg() paints a palette slot, falling back to the terminal's own palette.
  • Bash cards show incremental Python-like pseudocode with a Raw toggle, failure marks and variable values; the inspector adds layers, split source and timestamp replay. Native hosts draw an inline segmented run box (shell = command, omp.tool.bash.output = output).
  • VersionUpdate, WelcomeComponent.setVersionUpdate() and ComposerWelcomeUpdate.versionUpdate: the welcome banner shows background self-update progress, readiness or failure.
  • newSessionThinkingLevel / resolveConfiguredThinkingLevel resolve a new session's starting thinking level; ComposerCache.writeStatus accepts { shared: false } to keep a project's status out of the any-project fallback.
  • SettingItem.menu exposes a cycling row's choices as a menu opened by SettingsList.openSubmenuFor; Enter/Space still cycle.
  • StatusLineComponent.setSessionPinned() / setPinnedSessionIds() / isSessionPinned() track pinned sessions; session_name shows the theme's pin icon for them (#17848 by @SuperUserA)
  • Extension transcript presenters (setTranscriptPresenters, readTranscriptPresentation, TranscriptPresentationState in chat/transcript-presentation) draw tool cards and read entries full, compact or hidden (#17960 by @materemias)
  • PresentedBlock applies transcript presenters to advisor, skill, execution, background-job, TTSR and other non-tool blocks; TtsrNotificationComponent.rules exposes its merged rules (#17997 by @materemias)
  • LinkBrowserComponent (overlays/link-browser) browses a transcript fullscreen with stable link hint tags; LinkHints / viewportLinkTargets() share tagging with Composer; ChatTranscriptBuilder accepts textRenderers (#16476 by @materemias)
  • cache status-line segment with warm/cold indicators, plus StatusLineSession.getPromptCacheStatus, icon.cacheWarm / icon.cacheCold, the omp.composer.cache fact and promptCacheBrief / promptCacheDetails helpers (#12677) (#17496 by @will-bogusz)
  • ^ model mentions suggest supported effort levels after : and refresh as you type via optional AutocompleteProvider.trySyncModelMentionCompletion, so Tab accepts the current row (#17574 by @a-glapinski)
  • Shared spinner clock (spinner-clock: setSpinnerInterval, sharedSpinnerFrame, spinnerTickDelay) ticks all spinners together; 0 makes them static (#14240 by @mccraigmccraig)
  • Frame-rate ceiling (frame-rate: setMaxFps, minFrameIntervalMs) for rendering and animations; frames awaited by a keystroke keep 30 fps (#14240 by @mccraigmccraig)
  • setMotionEffects (motion-effects) turns off decorative motion: status-line blink, brand fade, magic-keyword sweep and native fx spans (#14240 by @mccraigmccraig)
  • CompactTranscriptBlock (chrome/transcript-container): consecutive blocks whose isTranscriptBlockCompact() is true render without blank rows between them, in terminal and native (TSP) surfaces (#17997 by @materemias)

Changed

  • Ask cards drop per-question section headers; each question shows its id and flags inline ((db · 3 options)) (#18104 by @JustasMasiulis)
  • Tab in the /usage Details view switches back to Overview (#17962 by @SuperUserA)
  • LSP results use one header with request and counts, preview each reference's source line, and syntax-highlight previews and definition snippets (#18106 by @JustasMasiulis)
  • Grep and AST Grep headers show the search scope after the pattern, errors show the tool title and pattern, and collapsed AST Grep drops meta: rows (#18105 by @JustasMasiulis)
  • AST Edit's header shows the search scope; the proposed state and replacement counts move to the card's bottom border (#18105 by @JustasMasiulis)
  • Centered sheets and dialogs on narrow native terminals fill the pane only once their content reaches half the screen height.

Fixed

  • Tern acknowledgements and visibility reports no longer appear as text in the editor when they arrive during a paste, including through Windows ConPTY.
  • The find card marks searches cut short by its 20 s budget as timed out (#18161 by @will-bogusz)
  • Tern's classic renderer no longer leaves stale inline images over text while an answer streams or the pane resizes.
  • The native composer's first frame shows the cached thinking/effort level instead of the plain model icon.
  • Clicking a many-valued enum setting's popup on native settings pages now opens its menu.
  • Ask notes no longer carry over between successive single-select questions (#17979)
  • LSP references no longer render as diagnostics or hover cards when a line looks like a diagnostics summary or code fence (#18106 by @JustasMasiulis)
  • The LSP diagnostics card lists diagnostics per file, counts every file, and reports server failures instead of No issues (#18106 by @JustasMasiulis)
  • Closing an overlay that others opened over no longer makes them return focus to it (#16476)
  • ASCII tree branches are two columns wide (|-, '-), so child rows align like Unicode trees (#18103 by @JustasMasiulis)

@oh-my-pi/pi-utils

Added

  • Added getUpdateStateDir(), returning the auto-update state directory (~/.omp/update).
  • Added logger.setLogForwarder() and logger.emitForeign() to forward log records across threads, preserving context and timestamps.
  • Added expandTilde() to expand a leading ~ to the home directory.

Fixed

  • Fixed spawn/exec timeouts never firing when the command's root exited before spawn() returned, which let a descendant holding stdout or stderr stall output reads until it exited.
  • ptree commands with subreaper: true now reliably terminate all descendants on graceful shutdown.
  • fatal() in a worker thread now reaches the parent's error listeners instead of only exiting with code 1.
  • Fixed the terminal stderr guard leaking a copy of the terminal into processes spawned from native code.

@oh-my-pi/pi-wire

Added

  • Added shell trace visualization: ShellTrace model and TSP shell kind for shell runs, node state, and layout.
  • Code views gain score-based line shading, labeled collapsible folds with notes, tones, and hover cards, and an overview strip.

Imbad0202/academic-research-skills ↗ 1 release · 1 stable
v3.24.2v3.24.2 — An honest null for experiment locks, repro_lock alignment, and a corrected sr-screener methods sentence Stable2026-11-06T04:38:21Z

Repairs and docs; no new skill or mode, and the prompt-level changes are unmeasured: An experiment_provenance[] entry can now say it has no honest lock values, with repro_lock: null and a repro_lock_reason (#1049). A survey or an analysis with no LLM could pass before only with placeholder values. Earlier ARS releases reject this form, so a passport that uses it needs this release. check_repro_lock.py now fails a required value that breaks its field's type or value rule, such as a null model id; cross_model.secondary_model_id may still be null. planned_vs_executed[].value accepts only a number or a string, so some passports that passed before now fail (#1050). The sr-screener methods text no longer says the reviewers could read only the record file: the tool list removes web and write access, and reading only the given file is an instruction (#1039). SETUP records two checks in which an uploaded skill dispatched a built-in sub-agent in a conversation after a retry; multi-agent stages stay untested (#1040).

Fixed

  • An experiment with no honest lock values can say so (#1049, PR #1051). Every experiment_provenance[] entry had to fill every repro_lock field, and those fields describe LLM and ARS run settings. A survey, an analysis with no LLM, or a trained model with no ARS prompts could pass only with placeholder values. The key stays required. Its value may now be null, and repro_lock_reason is then required; a reason beside a populated lock is rejected. The reason is free text: it discloses the gap, does not prove the lock does not apply, and no script checks the details it names. The passport-level validator and the required-field set do not change. Compatibility: earlier ARS releases reject repro_lock: null in an entry, so a passport that uses it needs this release.
  • repro_lock docs, example, gate, and validators agree (#1050, PR #1052). check_repro_lock.py now checks each required value against its field's type or value rule, matching the nested entry schema. cross_model.secondary_model_id may be null or any string, and the other string fields must be non-empty. A drift test compares the two on required keys, supported values, and a fixed set of wrong and edge-case values, not on every possible value, and extra keys stay the one known difference. The example passport gains a passport-level lock, and its two trained-model experiments use repro_lock: null with a reason. The D7 text in the integrity gate names 3.12.0 as the boundary and adds a fifth FAIL condition, a post-#260 passport with status legacy_unknown. The pattern doc no longer says a version change alters the prompt hashes, because they hash only the prompt files. Compatibility: a populated lock with a required value that breaks these rules now fails when check_repro_lock.py is run on it, and planned_vs_executed[].value accepts only a number or a string. Some passports that passed before now fail. The gate text is prompt-level and its effect is unmeasured.
  • The sr-screener methods text states what the tool list enforces (#1039, PR #1043). tools: Read, Grep removes web and write access but does not confine Read and Grep to one file. The title/abstract and full-text methods text now say: "The reviewer agents had read-only file tools and no web access; each was instructed to read only the file it was given." Methods text generated by an earlier release keeps the old sentence, so an author who used it should correct it by hand.

Docs

  • SETUP records what an uploaded skill did on Claude Desktop (#1040, PR #1045). Claude Desktop refused an upload named deep-research because Anthropic already provides a skill with that name, so Method 3 (en/zh-TW) now zips a copy named ars-deep-research, with the skill folder at the top of the zip. In two checks on 2026-11-02, the uploaded skill dispatched a built-in Explore sub-agent in a conversation; each time the first dispatch did not run and a retry did. Method 4a drops its 200-character and no-sub-agent claims and points to Method 3. docs/CONTROL_AVAILABILITY.md marks Cowork subagent orchestration and sr-screener dual review as Untested and adds note 12; a single dispatch is not multi-agent orchestration.
  • docs/SETUP.zh-TW.md and README.zh-TW.md drop their double em dashes (PR #1044). The 14 marks became colons, commas, or parentheses, with no content change.

heygen-com/hyperframes ↗ 3 releases · 3 stable
v0.9.19 Stable2026-11-06T03:46:37Z

HyperFrames v0.9.19

Released on 2026-11-06.

A render now matches preview when a set interrupts a running tween, so it no longer comes out blank. cloud render also keeps going when a project uses a font with no file on this computer. It falls back to the next font in the stack, as a local render does, and names the missing font.

Fixes

  • CLI: A font missing from this computer no longer stops a cloud render, and is named (b56e2c036, #7287).
  • Core: A render lands where preview does when a set interrupts a tween (6abc62a17, #7283).

v0.9.18 Stable2026-11-06T01:41:41Z

HyperFrames v0.9.18

Released on 2026-11-05.

A render on a busy Windows PC no longer stalls behind its own lint. Under heavy CPU load in our test, the wait before the browser check fell from over 30 seconds to about 3. check also stops piling up GPU memory on graded images, and normalize-audio now reports the level a held clip actually stays at.

Fixes

  • CLI: A render's lint runs at normal priority, so a busy Windows PC no longer stalls it (72326a3ac, #7268).
  • CLI: Normalize-audio says where the target stays when a limit holds its gain (4935d6736, #7267).
  • Core: Check's no-draw seeks no longer upload graded images to the GPU (256b84a41, #7264).

v0.9.17 Stable2026-11-05T20:10:34Z

HyperFrames v0.9.17

Released on 2026-11-05.

Set HF_VIDEO_FRAME_WINDOW=true with segmented capture and a long render holds far less on disk. Each segment extracts only the frames it shows, then deletes them. Segmented capture also checks drawElement frames against their reference samples now, and check --budget stops a whole check in time.

Features

  • Producer: Render long videos with windowed source frames behind a flag (968f97fe9, #7242).
  • Producer: A capture segment extracts the frames it shows and deletes them (599eaad2b, #7241).
  • CLI: Check --budget stops the whole check in time and reports what it found (b7d72c947, #7245).
  • CLI: Info --media lists every clip the render hears, on the film's clock (df54f380c, #7235).

Fixes

  • CLI: Check judges SVG gradient text by its gradient stops, not as black (478ef2f26, #7258).
  • Engine: Name a failed script's origin and error before the free text of its warning (7290ee68c, #7256).
  • CLI: Normalize-audio --reference stops short of the peak ceiling instead of refusing (2f2195515, #7254).
  • Producer: Segmented and sequential capture check drawElement samples (8df4f21c2, #7255).
  • Core: Two scenes drawing SVGs with the same ids each show their own (b108f8512, #7251).
  • CLI: End a stalled cloud upload and send it again (f98218a22, #7237).
  • Core: Decode WebKit sources by memory budget, not by length (600bfa451, #7232).
  • Core: Snapshot and preview show a clip on the frame render shows it (5377b756b, #7238).
  • Producer: An export over the peak ceiling loses only what it must (d40d0b251, #7246).
  • Lint: Every selector in a comma list counts as hidden by authored CSS (a664f99ec, #7252).
  • Core: The bundle's contract check reads a timeline registered in a local script (0b3559824, #7248).
  • CLI: Check sees an SVG stroke drawing on, so it no longer calls it a dead stretch (644b166e7, #7250).
  • CLI: An unknown command answers in a few lines and says what to run instead (0a6746e1d, #7247).
  • CLI: Lint says nothing when the OS refuses to lower its priority (c1977fab3, #7244).
  • Lint: Compositions in .hyperframesignore no longer count as the project's (80e02e74a, #7239).
  • CLI: Name the network cause of a failed fetch in the error box (f55990ebc, #7236).
  • Core: Hlg footage tone-maps to sdr at the bt.2408 reference white (5ee9e928d, #7234).

Internal

  • Producer: Deferred frame extraction moves out of the distributed code (35cbca992, #7240).

mksglu/context-mode ↗ 2 releases · 2 stable
v2.1.4 Stable2026-11-05T22:06:22Z

Security

  • Deny rules now apply to every shell language of ctx_execute (sh, bash, zsh, dash, ksh, fish) and to ctx_batch commands, checked per command segment like the Bash tool. The deny check now runs in linear time, so long input stays inside the hook budget. (bcd2921)
  • Fetch blocks more private and special address ranges (shared 100.64.0.0/10, 0.0.0.0/8, 198.18.0.0/15, 240.0.0.0/4, fec0::/10, ::/96, 64:ff9b:1::/48), checks the IPv4 address inside NAT64 and 6to4 addresses, and checks every redirect hop. Stored line units now grow linearly with input size. (6de3349)
  • ctx_upgrade keeps v1 data: old v1 data dirs are moved aside and kept, and a path reached through a link is reported and left as is. The PostToolUse hook reads persisted output only from its own session dir, as a plain file of at most 16 MiB. (54c4e2d)

Fixes

  • fix: guard test keeps the Claude Code Agent input unchanged on first call and resend (#1319)
  • fix: setup writes the stable Homebrew node link, so brew upgrade node keeps hooks and MCP running
  • fix: ctx_upgrade keeps v1 data and never follows a project link; hook reads persisted output only from its own session dir
  • fix: VS Code server reads the workspace from MCP roots, setup writes no ${workspaceFolder} in the user mcp.json
  • fix: ZCode native plugin install, Qwen project scope and Windows hook command, hook overhead as warning
  • fix: ctx_upgrade uses each agent's own update command, no release-branch clone (#1309)
  • fix: block more private fetch targets and bound line unit size
  • fix: OpenCode 2 host (Bun): doctor hook run, background launcher, read-only DB open, D3 entries, read size guard (#1310)
  • fix: CONTEXT_MODE_DATA_DIR moves the data root, so each Claude Code profile can keep its own store (#1318)
  • fix: gemini native install, GEMINI_CLI_HOME, AfterTool replace wording, Part lists
  • fix: apply user deny rules to every shell ctx_execute language, per segment, in linear time

Who gets this

One version on both channels: the release commit on main (GitHub) and npm.

NousResearch/hermes-agent ↗ 1 release · 1 stable
v0.23.0Hermes Agent v0.23.0 — The Hearth Release Stable2026-11-05T21:32:33Z

Hermes Agent v0.23.0

Release Date: November 5, 2026 Since v0.22.0: ~10,080 commits · ~1,950 merged PRs · ~8,720 files changed · ~725,200 insertions · ~201,400 deletions · ~850 issues closed · 666 contributors

The Hearth Release. In the old hymns Hermes kept the threshold and Hestia kept the hearth, and the two were honored side by side. v0.22.0 got newcomers through the door; v0.23.0 builds the fire they gather around. The headline: your whole Desktop workspace now opens in a browser tab, served by your own gateway. Underneath it, every conversation lives in one place: a single gateway owns each session, and the CLI, TUI, Desktop, editors, bots and cron all sit down at the same table instead of each running its own agent. From that one hearth, Desktop keeps every machine you run Hermes on connected at once, Group Chats route their own conversations, and the terminal finally draws your images inline and lets you rewind a conversation. Around it: plugins that keep themselves updated, price tags and spend limits on paid MCP tools, pluggable delegation, a plugin catalog that grew from about 600 to over 1,050 entries, a leaner built-in skill set, and a long run of memory and latency work for long-lived gateways. This release rolls up and fully documents the v0.22.1–v0.22.7 patch releases.

<!-- HERMES_BUILDS_TABLE -->

✨ Highlights

  • Your Desktop workspace in a browser tab — The new Webapp serves the full Desktop workspace from your own gateway: the same chats, Group Chats, plugins and settings, in any browser on your network or behind a public URL with sign-in. The Docker image and Nix package ship it. It is off by default; turn it on with webapp.enabled or Web Access in Settings. (#141864, #145598, #145536, #145072, #145153 — @jquesnelle, @BearHuddleston, @IAvecilla, @Adolanium)

  • One gateway owns every conversation — Before, the CLI, TUI, Desktop, editors (ACP), messaging bots and cron could each run their own copy of the agent against the same chat, which is how messages got written twice or went missing between surfaces. Now each machine runs one gateway that owns every session, and every surface attaches to it as a client: a chat you open anywhere is the same chat, with one queue and one history. You can even steer or edit a queued prompt from Desktop without it running twice. (#106742, #140334 — @unsupportedpastels, @kshitijk4poor)

  • Pictures in the terminal — When you paste an image, or a tool takes a screenshot, the CLI and TUI now draw it right in the chat instead of printing a file path: real pixels in kitty and Ghostty (CLI and TUI) and in iTerm2, WezTerm and Konsole (CLI), coloured blocks everywhere else, and full resolution in VS Code's terminal when its image support is on. (#147293, #148874 — @Adolanium)

  • Rewind a conversation — Press Esc twice or type /rewind in the TUI or classic CLI, pick an earlier message, then edit and resend it or fork a new session from that point. The original session stays exactly as it was. The TUI can also start /new while a turn is still running; the old one keeps working in the background. (#144937, #148880 — @SaguaroDev)

  • Every machine in one Desktop window — Desktop now keeps all your gateways connected at once, so switching between this computer and an SSH or remote gateway is instant. Terminal tabs run on the host they were opened on, and a Group Chat can mix bots from different machines. (#147681, #147708, #148418 — @dskwe, @janviernine)

  • Group Chats that run themselves — Bot Mode rooms can let a lead bot, or an automatic router, pick who answers a message that names nobody. Scheduled jobs can post into a room, rooms keep a purpose and shared notes, and bots no longer ping each other in endless loops. Rooms also look like a normal Hermes chat now, and each bot shows what it is doing live ("scout · terminal · 7s"). (#144833, #148417, #148888, #148903, #148422 — @KoNit-K)

  • Plugins that keep themselves current — hermes plugins update updates everything at once, and an opt-in Auto-update switch (CLI, chat, Desktop and dashboard) keeps catalog and git plugins up to date. Updates that would break your setup are held back instead of applied. The catalog itself grew from about 600 to over 1,050 entries, and a new Verified tier marks plugins built by the vendor itself. (#141513, #85794, #143468, #144257 — @charan-rathore, @YS-OH-CORE)

  • Price tags and spend limits for paid tools — Paid MCP tools, such as 3D or media generation, can declare what a call costs. The approval prompt shows each call's price and the chat's running total, and Hermes asks before a chat goes over its limit, even in YOLO mode. (#142470, #143780, #143795, #143787 — @liuhao1024, @fangliquanflq)

  • New official plugins, and a smaller core — Meshy (text and image to 3D), ElevenLabs Music and Sound Effects, X Chat, REA (reverse engineering) and a bubblewrap sandbox are now one-command installs. Camofox, mcp-serve, the agent importers and Singularity moved out of core into plugins that install themselves for anyone who used them. (#142619, #146294, #139725, #143484, #139983)

  • Choose how subagents are run — Delegation is now pluggable: keep the default, or install the official advanced-delegation plugin to send each subagent task to a named model or into a separate profile. Switch with /delegation. (#142563, #142564, #143423)

  • Lighter and faster, especially when Hermes runs all day — Recurring cron jobs share a prompt cache, one-shot runs send about 1,570 fewer prompt tokens per call, and patch/write_file return in milliseconds with LSP on. Long-lived gateways now let go of closed sessions' memory, and the Desktop sidebar no longer slows down as sessions pile up (one store with ~35k cron runs went from a 121 s refresh to instant). (#139876, #144177, #140387, #147863, #137087, #147819, #144940 — @kshitijk4poor)

  • A leaner skill set you can lock — The built-in skills went from 58 to 44, with Word, PowerPoint and Excel merged into one microsoft-office skill. hermes curator lock stops the agent from rewriting a skill you curated by hand, through any tool, even in YOLO mode. (#147557 — @daviseford)

  • Password managers and a keychain vault key — Verified password-manager plugins can hand the browser your logins without seeing anyone else's, and the browser vault's key can now live in your OS keychain. (#143964, #144246 — @Jesssullivan)

  • Stop a running cron job — Cancel a running job from the CLI, Desktop, the API or the agent itself, and set per job what happens to runs missed while Hermes was off. (#144801 — @kokhlo, @fangliquanflq, @LeonSGP43)

⚠️ Upgrade notes

  • The old built-in browser tools are gone. Browser automation runs on browser_exec only; agent-browser and the ten browser_* tools (browser_navigate, browser_click, ...) were removed. Homes that used Camofox get the camofox plugin installed automatically. If your own prompts, skills or toolset lists name the old tools, switch them to browser_exec. (#143146)
  • Desktop needs macOS 13+ and 64-bit Windows. The app moved to Electron 44, which no longer runs on macOS 11, macOS 12 or 32-bit Windows. (#142727 — @OutThisLife, @Adihaliem)
  • hermes mcp serve is now hermes mcp-serve (it moved into a plugin). MCP clients that launch Hermes as a server need their command updated; nothing rewrites it for you.
  • Word, PowerPoint and Excel skills are now one microsoft-office skill. Old copies stay on disk until you remove them.
  • The Webapp is off by default. Nothing is served to a browser until you turn on webapp.enabled or Web Access.

🧠 Core Agent, Gateway & Tools

Providers & Models

  • GLM-5.3 is the default model when you never pick one — The silent default moves from GLM-5.2 to GLM-5.3, at about the same price and with the same 1M context window on Nous Portal and OpenRouter. (#144810 — @calvinnwq)
  • New models — GPT-6.1 Terra, DeepSeek V4.1 Flash 1017 and Gemini 4 Argon on Solstice. Claude Mythos is treated as always-thinking, so reasoning-off requests stop failing their first try. Nemotron 3.5 Super VL gets default parameters. (#145204, #140496, #139466, #148127, #138462 — @benbarclay, @unsupportedpastels, @rroverin)
  • Usage-window plans — On subscriptions that track usage as allowances instead of credits, /usage shows one bar per allowance with its reset time. Pickers list the plan's recommended models first, tag them Large or Free, and say when an allowance has run out. (#142308, #145682 — @rob-maron)
  • Daybreak for approved ChatGPT subscriptions — An off-by-default agent.daybreak switch turns on OpenAI's Trusted Access for Cyber program. It only appears on models that your account's own Codex catalog lists as approved, and it is checked again on every request. (#148056 — @unsupportedpastels, @Froraut)
  • /model reset — Takes a session pinned with /model back to your configured default without losing the conversation, in the CLI, TUI, Desktop and messaging. (#80448 — @shannonsands)
  • GitHub Copilot moved to a labs plugin — Copilot (copilot, copilot-acp) now lives in the github-copilot Hermes Labs plugin, which profiles that use Copilot install automatically. Core keeps generic provider hooks that any provider plugin can use. (#144788)
  • Custom endpoints resolve reliably — New profiles inherit custom:<name>, ${VAR} and legacy custom endpoints instead of failing with "Unknown provider". Resumed chats stay on the endpoint that served them, and a built-in provider's key no longer leaks onto a custom entry with the same name. (#138513, #139947, #140335, #147761 — @jquesnelle, @unsupportedpastels)
  • Provider quirks handled — Streamed tool calls from LM Studio, Kimi and MiniMax no longer split into broken calls. Gemini safety blocks now show as a content-policy error and move on to the fallback model. Text-only vLLM servers that reject images recover, and byte-cap 400s trigger compression. On Bedrock, a hung call fails after one read timeout instead of five. (#140485, #85031, #85005, #85126, #146314 — @Chukwuebuka-2003)
  • Local runtime on llama.cpp b11671 — Adds one-slot DFlash drafts and opt-in Nemotron 3.5 Bolt. (#139703 — @jquesnelle)

Agent Loop & Sessions

  • Lower prompt-token and cache costs — Every fire of the same cron job now shares one prompt-cache key. Background one-shot calls (summaries, titles, /moa) stop paying for cache writes. One-shot runs send 1,570 fewer prompt tokens per call, and four tool schemas are 319 tokens shorter. Gateway goal and heartbeat turns, post-compaction turns and between-turn MCP refreshes no longer rewrite the cached prefix. (#139876, #140478, #144177, #144173, #139865, #139851 — @100yenadmin, @jonpol01, @chelsealong, @AdrianBinDC)
  • Opt-in prepared compaction — With compression.prepare_ahead: true, the next summary is written in the background as a session nears the trigger, so compaction no longer pauses the turn while the summary is written. Messages that arrive in the meantime stay in the tail word for word. Off by default. (#144684, #144886 — @kshitijk4poor)
  • Compaction is more honest and harder to stall — Summaries list uncertain claims on UNVERIFIED: lines, so a guess does not come back as a fact. The main-model fallback uses the main route, /stop closes a summary stream, and queued messages and /steer survive a context-exhaustion reset. (#86019, #139872, #144825, #139783, #139923 — @helix4u, @thehugegatsby)
  • hermes sessions refresh-prompts — Continuing sessions pick up an edited SOUL.md, skills or context files on their next turn without /new. History is kept and each session takes one cache miss. (#143581 — @victor-kyriazakos)
  • Opt-in stream lifetime cap — agent.stream_max_lifetime (off by default) cuts off a stream that never finishes. Timeout errors now name the phase that failed and the setting to raise. (#80701, #80873 — @krzsda)
  • Follow-up questions about a photo still see the photo — This applies after --resume, API-server session continuation and gateway restarts. (#139873 — @Alexbob0)
  • Session storage — Auto-prune is opt-in again, and Hermes asks once when the disk runs low. hermes sessions optimize-storage shows progress and finished a real store about 18x faster. Startup no longer waits on the scratch prune, and hermes sessions failures exit 1. (#139762, #144630, #144625, #144767)

Messaging Platforms (Gateway)

  • One gateway now owns every local session — Since v0.22.0, the classic CLI, TUI, Desktop, API, ACP editors, bots and cron all run their sessions through one gateway. This cycle fixed what that broke: slash commands, /retry, /refine, /review, clarify cards, reasoning display, usage and ACP sessions work again, and config changes reach resumed sessions. (#139149, #139577, #139884, #140249, #140595, #141916, #144005, #138719 — @unsupportedpastels, @yoniebans, @drzxk, @fangliquanflq)
  • Gateway memory and responsiveness — The roughly once-a-minute freeze during memory trims is gone, closed sessions leave memory, glibc malloc arenas are capped at 4, and measured event-loop blockers moved off the loop. (#147840, #147831, #147810, #144911, #137156 — @kouyichi, @ygd58, @zwmaupin, @kshitijk4poor)
  • Safer gateway lifecycle — hermes gateway stop/restart refuse while sessions are live and never act on another home's service. A gateway started from an agent's terminal no longer mistakes itself for the launchd service, and when every platform fails at start the gateway stays up degraded. (#142041, #145930, #144592, #140922 — @salch-cred)
  • Bot Mode Group Chats — Cron jobs can post into a room (deliver: room:<id>@<profile>), waking only the bots they @mention. Rooms get a purpose and shared notes, and two opt-in modes pick who answers a message that names nobody (a lead bot, or auto routing). message_agent wakes the asker once with every reply. (#144833, #148417, #148888, #144789 — @rorsch, @OutThisLife)
  • Bot-to-bot loops end — Bots never receive runtime notices, and an exchange is capped at max_hops (default 4) after the last human message. hermes peer dm <peer>/hermes reaches the peer's default bot. (#148903, #144900 — @KoNit-K)
  • Telegram — New opt-in dm_topic_auto_transfer opens a fresh topic for root-DM messages. A new topic's first message lands in it, video notes reach the agent, and group messages not addressed to the bot can stay silent. (#141347, #141751, #147572, #147144 — @KoNit-K, @PRELVAS94, @sharpnotebot, @Horizont367, @diegoasanch)
  • Slack, Feishu, Discord — Slack thread context includes emoji reactions, and a new opt-in thread_followup_silence covers unaddressed thread replies. Feishu gets clarify cards and a choice picker you tap. Discord honours longer approval timeouts and sends plain audio as an attachment. Rooms can opt into intentional silence. (#80882, #139586, #82170, #145948, #143800, #147143 — @hackartists, @helix4u, @liuhao1024, @nicodoggie)
  • Every messaging adapter is now a plugin — Signal, Weixin, BlueBubbles, QQ Bot, Yuanbao and WhatsApp Cloud now ship as bundled platform plugins. Nothing changes for users. (#139843, #140168 — @karljohannisson, @sprmn24)
  • Streamed replies deliver images — Markdown images in a streamed reply arrive as pictures, and tool-progress arguments render in code blocks. (#144041, #139759 — @686f6c61, @sambouli)

Tools, Browser & Terminal

  • patch and write_file return in milliseconds with LSP on — A clean TypeScript edit went from 10.1 s to about 0.06 s. Type errors still reach the model. (#140387)
  • Smarter background processes — A command ending in a lone & runs as a tracked background process instead of being refused. Instead of polling a quiet process, the agent is notified when it exits. Jobs that finish during a busy turn come back as one turn. (#140317, #140330, #147678 — @lucasfdale, @suhanrain)
  • Password managers in browser_vault — Bundled or verified/official catalog plugins can add their own logins through ctx.register_login_backend, and each one can touch only its own entries. The vault key can move into the OS keychain (opt-in vault.key_storage: keychain). Saved passwords fill on Camofox pages. (#143964, #144246, #146240, #139203 — @Jesssullivan, @WillPlatnick)
  • Media generation — Krea 3 image generation and editing through the Nous gateway or the Krea API, and the default image model for paid subscribers who have not picked one. Wan 2.7 video on FAL. The agent can search and pick ElevenLabs voices in text_to_speech. (#142155, #87464, #144633 — @entropidelic)
  • Terminal backends — Sandboxing backends can set uses_host_filesystem = False so the file tools run inside the sandbox, and plugin backends can set their start directory. Singularity moved to its plugin. Command output no longer vanishes once a process has more than 1024 open file descriptors. (#143756, #142225, #142561, #142299 — @unsupportedpastels, @aholten, @MohamadKanso)
  • Browser reliability — The real-profile setting keeps working when Chrome locks the profile. Nested page data no longer crashes the tools, and hidden-tab screenshots no longer hang. (#139245, #140562, #146082 — @cmaldonado80)

Cron, Delegation & Multi-Agent

  • Pluggable delegation backends — delegation.backend chooses how each delegate_task task becomes a child run, and the default strategy is now a bundled plugin. The official advanced-delegation plugin adds model-map and per-profile routing. /delegation <mode> saves for the next session, and --now applies it immediately. An opt-in delegation.prefix_stagger_seconds lets batch subagents reuse the prompt cache. (#142563, #142564, #143423, #86553)
  • Stop a running cron job — hermes cron cancel, a Desktop Stop button, the API and cronjob_manage stop a live run, and deleting a job cancels it. Each job picks its own missed-run policy (--catch-up on|off). A manual run no longer consumes a one-shot, and prompts carry the fire time. (#144801, #146113, #82826 — @kokhlo, @fangliquanflq, @LeonSGP43)
  • Background subagents survive and report back — Stopping a turn no longer kills background subagents like /review. /bg and routed chats wait for their delegations, and completions that were lost are offered again from the ledger. (#144759, #147745, #147753, #147748 — @professorpalmer, @nateEc, @rungmc357)
  • Kanban workers are usable again — delegate_task and session_search work inside cards, dead attempts free their slot, a worker whose socket drops resumes the same attempt, and worker turns are no longer saved as your memories. (#141944, #141906, #145954, #144861 — @tmaarcxs, @nonkun12, @calvinnwq)

Security & Reliability

  • More secret shapes are masked — Quoted secrets, connection strings, glued and flag-passed secrets, Azure keys, non-PEM private keys and SMB passwords are now masked, and JSON pages are no longer mangled. (#149435, #148862, #149470 — @r266-tech, @liuhao1024, @mtp-44, @arbianshkodra, @Baophan00)
  • Approval gate catches more — The gate now prompts for curl … | sudo bash pipes, later or abbreviated PowerShell deletes, and credential uploads hidden behind an approved rule. A stale approval card can no longer approve the next request. (#87362, #148865, #141377, #87554 — @Baophan00, @liuhao1024, @VaughnValle)
  • Network boundaries — URLs whose host parses differently across clients are refused, and without a token A2A refuses requests from browsers and from foreign Host headers. (#145578, #145310 — @unsupportedpastels)
  • Shell-hook consent — An unattended gateway skips an unapproved hook instead of freezing. A recorded consent takes effect within about 30 seconds. (#140984, #141034 — @arcabotai, @WYSTAF, @liuhao1024)

🖥️ Desktop, Webapp, CLI & Install

Desktop App

  • Every registered gateway stays connected — Switching between This device, an SSH host or a URL gateway is now instant, with no confirm dialog and no blanked session list. Every gateway's sessions stay visible in the sidebar, and pins stay with the gateway that owns them. An SSH gateway runs one remote backend for all of its profiles, so every SSH profile is listed at launch and switching takes well under a second. (#147681, #147735, #147741, #147957, #147711 — @dskwe, @Bridgerz, @kenpritchard, @kshitijk4poor)
  • Terminal tabs belong to their host — Each in-app terminal tab runs on the gateway it was opened on and shows that host in its label. "New terminal on…" opens a shell on This device or any SSH gateway, side by side. Desktop can also connect to Windows SSH hosts. (#147708, #143898 — @janviernine, @addelh, @yoniebans)
  • Group Chats grew up — Group Chats can now be renamed and disbanded, and support @mentions, attachment paste, drag-and-drop and inline image previews. Rooms can seat bots from different machines again and show which bot is working. Desktop and the Webapp share one room list. (#143607, #148418, #142904, #145629, #141928, #145973 — @unsupportedpastels, @HootRock, @Thrymheim, @Ehsan-hash)
  • Delegation settings in the app — Settings → Model → Subagents now uses provider and model dropdowns instead of free-text boxes. A new Routing section picks the delegation mode (Default, Model routes, Profile routes, or a plugin backend) and edits routes in place. (#141312, #143126 — @abundantbeing, @e-macgregor, @SecBySagar)
  • Redesigned Capabilities catalog — Skills and Plugins are browsed as card shelves ordered by tier (Official, Verified, Community). Install is now a one-way + button: installed entries get an on/off switch, and Uninstall moves to the detail view. (#138820, #142708 — @austinpickett)
  • Plugin SDK: file access and Kanban badges — The new ctx.files lets a Desktop plugin read and write its own data folder and any folder you grant through the app's picker, and nothing else. A Kanban card badge slot lets plugins label cards without touching the board's markup. (#143688, #143673)
  • Clearer errors and questions — Failed turns show a redesigned error card that marks the failure as blocking or degraded and offers one action (Continue, Open provider settings, Setup guide…). It shows the real exception instead of "Something went wrong". A clarify with several questions is now one form with one submit. (#146776, #146803, #144392, #146881 — @kshitijk4poor)
  • Workflow conveniences — You can drag sessions to pin or unpin them, pin Kanban boards as quick-switch tabs, open Bot Chats in new tabs, and compact a conversation from the context panel. /loop shows a live countdown, and /usage shows provider limits before the first message. (#142672, #148179, #143129, #148871, #139187, #141047 — @austinpickett, @unsupportedpastels, @kvarnelis, @jerrygooch, @aydnOktay)
  • MCP consent shows what will run — The MCP install/enable/authorize card now shows the exact command or URL each server will run or contact, with secrets masked. Approval prompts on every surface now reveal hidden Unicode, control bytes and padding. (#85775, #81403)
  • Faster on long chats — Typing no longer slows as history loads; sessions full of file edits switch without re-highlighting every diff. Mermaid diagrams render through Pele, a much smaller renderer, instead of mermaid.js. Minimized and idle windows do less streaming work. (#144903, #144991, #147974, #146602, #146600 — @kshitijk4poor, @ghgoodreau)
  • Messages and replies you can trust — A message sent just before closing the window is no longer lost, and drafts survive clock jumps and downgrades. Replies are drawn once across reconnects, reloads and stopped turns. Healthy connections are no longer dropped on focus or wake. Old phone-app chats can be continued instead of opening read-only. (#145018, #145528, #144899, #146843, #147802)
  • Language packs actually apply — Korean and other pack-only languages now load on a cold gateway, and the picker hides languages the app cannot show. (#147515 — @kshitijk4poor)

Webapp & Hermes Cloud

  • The Webapp: Desktop in a browser tab, served by your gateway — With webapp.enabled: true, the gateway serves the Desktop workspace on its own listener. hermes webapp opens it, and Desktop's Settings → Web Access serves it on your network or a public URL behind sign-in. It is off by default. The Webapp is single-user: a signed-in tab acts as you, so its sessions and Group Chats are the same ones native Desktop shows, and display settings follow you across devices on the same machine. (#141864, #145598, #145536, #148408 — @jquesnelle, @BearHuddleston, @IAvecilla)
  • Desktop plugins in the browser — Installed Desktop plugins load in a Webapp tab, and desktop-only plugins can be installed from the tab, pinned to the commit the dialog showed. (#145072, #145853, #146178 — @Adolanium, @DECRUX9812)
  • Phone and browser polish — When a chat needs you, the tab title flashes and the favicon gets a dot. Opt-in browser notifications open the right chat. One tap focuses the composer on iPhone, and the phone tab picker reaches every tab's menu. Files and media resolve inside a session's Docker sandbox or SSH workspace. (#146882, #145569, #145788, #144741 — @Sn0wfly, @unsupportedpastels)
  • Webapp hardening — Launch links are private to the signed-in user, and the page sends a Content-Security-Policy; a signed-out /api/status reveals no profile names. The Webapp, dashboard and hermes serve trust only their own bound loopback port for CORS and WebSockets. (#144721, #145573, #145931, #146636 — @unsupportedpastels, @dokterdok, @Froraut)
  • Hermes Cloud agents stop offering in-app updates — Desktop and the Webapp no longer show "Update now" for an agent only Cloud can update. (#149436)

CLI & TUI

  • One gateway owns every local session — The classic CLI, TUI, Desktop, API server, ACP, bots and cron now attach to each profile's single gateway instead of running their own agents against the same database. A chat is one conversation with one queue and one writer. Follow-ups route classic CLI commands (/model, /cron, /queue, /save, /snapshot, /resume, todos) through it. (#106742, #138717, #141105, #141501, #141962, #140676 — @unsupportedpastels, @kshitijk4poor, @kassdinzheng123)
  • Rewind to an earlier prompt — In the TUI and the classic CLI, /rewind (or Esc Esc on an empty prompt) lists your earlier messages. You can edit one and resend it, or fork a new session from just before it. Approval prompts gain "No, and tell Hermes what to do instead". (#144937 — @SaguaroDev)
  • TUI: /new mid-turn and expandable pastes — /new while Hermes is busy opens a fresh session and keeps the running one working in the background (Ctrl+X switches back). Ctrl+P expands a collapsed paste so you can read or edit it. (#148880, #148883)
  • New classic CLI layout — The classic CLI now prints a transcript that survives terminal resizes, with one glyph per row and the status line under the composer. display.cli_layout: classic restores the old look. (#139241, #141879)
  • Smaller core, setup imports — hermes mcp serve moves to the official mcp-serve plugin (hermes mcp-serve). hermes import-agent and the OpenClaw migration move to the agent-importers plugin. First-time hermes setup now offers to import from OpenClaw, Claude Code, Codex CLI or Cursor when it finds one. (#142556, #142547)
  • hermes --cpu-profile PATH — Writes a CPU profile of any Hermes run, plus a top-functions text summary. (#87545)

Install, Update & Packaging

  • New installs start on stable — install.sh, install.ps1 and Hermes-Setup now check out the latest stable release instead of the tip of main. (#139311)
  • Desktop updates are faster and say more — An update skips the Desktop rebuild when no desktop input changed, and the update window names its current stage. The dialog shows real release notes. A failed update shows the updater's output and offers a one-click diagnostics upload. Large state.db files, comma locales and the system proxy no longer break updates. (#144922, #138483, #148355, #144894 — @KoNit-K, @chenleshu)
  • hermes update got much more reliable — It no longer stashes untracked folders it never touches, and it asks before stashing anything. It reclaims git locks left by a killed update and no longer hangs on an in-flight kanban card. It also names a CDN firewall block instead of a bare 403. (#147632, #147954, #147958, #140023, #138420, #144507)
  • Electron 44 — Desktop moves from Electron 40 to 44. This ends Desktop support on macOS 11, macOS 12 and 32-bit Windows. (#142727 — @ethernet8023, @OutThisLife, @ahrazzle)
  • Webapp ships in Docker and Nix; Docker sandboxes give files back — The Docker image and the Nix package now ship the Webapp bundle. The image adds tmux and Office/PDF libraries. Files a root Docker sandbox writes are handed back to your user, and setuid bits and file capabilities are stripped from them. (#145153, #145521, #146111, #141033, #141086 — @IAvecilla, @unsupportedpastels)
  • Package manager — hermes pm install --without EXTRA drops a carried extra and keeps it dropped. A corrupt cached package no longer blocks every install, and pm update can skip a lagging target. Plugin dependency edge cases no longer fail the update lock. (#148194, #146929, #142657, #148199, #148205 — @benbarclay, @ethernet8023, @hteo1337)

Infrastructure & CI

  • Release pipeline — The Microsoft Store build now certifies before the release gate and goes live automatically at publication. Stable runs dispatch from the default branch so caches restore, and macOS notarization keeps going through dropped connections. (#141725, #148716, #138159, #148497 — @ethernet8023, @unsupportedpastels, @BearHuddleston)

🧩 Plugins, Skills & MCP

Plugins

  • Plugins keep themselves up to date — hermes plugins update with no name lists every installed plugin that has an update, asks once and updates them all (--yes for scripts). An Update all action and an Auto-update switch (plugins.auto_apply, off by default) now exist in the CLI and TUI (/plugins update, /plugins auto-update), Desktop and the web dashboard. Catalog installs now update too, and hermes update tells you when the new catalog has updates for them. An update whose scan comes back as caution shows you the findings and lets you go ahead (--force in the CLI, a dialog in Desktop). (#141513, #85794, #141894 — @alt-glitch)
  • Updates no longer break your setup — when hermes update has to turn a plugin off, it keeps your memory provider, turns the plugin back on once it fits again, and every surface shows it as off. Plugins whose dependencies have no wheels for an OS you don't run (mnemosyne, torch) no longer get disabled on every update. A plugin listed as both enabled and disabled is now reported by the loader and hermes doctor. (#144257, #144220, #144394 — @charan-rathore, @YS-OH-CORE, @renleding, @liuhao1024)
  • Catalog version floors are enforced — an entry's requires_hermes now blocks installs on a Hermes that is too old, and update won't move a plugin to a pin that needs a newer release. Plugins that use a manifest key from a newer Hermes install with a warning instead of failing. (#143468, #139225)
  • New Verified tier in the catalog — Nous gives this tier to plugins made by the company whose product they integrate. Cua Spaces, NVIDIA App, NVIDIA Broadcast, TinyFish and CoreWeave Inference are the first. Plugin pages now put disclosures in their own section at the bottom, and cards show what the plugin does. (#139983, #142165, #141742, #142184)
  • More features leave core for official plugins — Camofox, hermes mcp serve (now hermes mcp-serve, with a new --tools mode), hermes import-agent (adds Cursor, Grok Bot and OpenBot importers) and the Singularity/Apptainer backend all move to plugins. Homes that use Camofox, Singularity or import-agent get the plugin installed automatically; MCP clients have to change their args to mcp-serve. (#143484, #142545, #142538, #147799, #142536)
  • New official plugins — REA (reverse engineering), X Chat (encrypted X DMs as a gateway platform), Meshy (3D generation), ElevenLabs Sound Effects, ElevenLabs Music (songs, lyrics, stems, video scores), advanced-delegation, and bubblewrap (a Linux sandbox for terminal and code commands). Hermes Labs adds hermes inbox, hermes record (workflow recording that masks passwords) and Patsnap patent search. (#138225, #139725, #142619, #143744, #146294, #143265, #142548, #142523, #142519, #85138)
  • New plugin APIs — plugins can now post a message to the current chat and edit it in place (ctx.post_message), ask the session's own agent a question after a turn (ctx.side_question, which needs your consent), block a turn before it reaches the model (pre_llm_call block), and hold back streaming until a rewrite hook has run (hold_stream). New hooks: post_compaction and on_session_delete. Slash commands now receive the calling session, and platform plugins and plugin skill lookups get public helpers. (#141005, #143791, #81434, #144266, #141017, #140995, #140997, #140624, #140978 — @curiouscleo, @Halldrix, @Baophan00, @Finn763, @pieralukasz, @ewtillman, @nekwo)
  • Plugins can sign you in to third-party APIs without seeing the token — a plugin declares an OAuth client, hermes auth add <plugin>:<id> runs the browser sign-in, and Hermes attaches the token only on requests to the hosts the plugin declared. (#141510 — @bablobanov)
  • Bigger Desktop plugin SDK — Desktop plugins can add rows to the composer's right-click menu and words to the spell checker, render media from their own tools, post cards into their own chat, and script the preview Browser once they declare the capability and you allow it for that site. Portable plugins get a Settings ▸ Plugins form, and a newly saved API key reaches their MCP server without a restart. (#143678, #143980, #143776, #143668, #141533 — @Schildkrote)
  • Plugins behave the same on a shared gateway — classic CLI plugin commands and pre_command now run in the gateway with the rest of the plugin, and disabling a plugin also closes its websockets. A secondary profile's dashboard plugins run in that profile's own plugin host. (#139575, #139691, #139569, #139689 — @unsupportedpastels, @dokterdok)
  • Remote config backend for fleets (opt-in) — a fleet agent such as one on Hermes Cloud can load each profile's config.yaml from a central service, and admins can lock keys. It only turns on when HERMES_CONFIG_BACKEND=remote is set. (#122509 — @benbarclay)
  • Faster startup — from a git checkout every hermes command starts about 1 s faster, because provider plugins and the restart check no longer run git each time (15 git calls down to 2). (#144640 — @100yenadmin)

Skills

  • Lock a skill — after hermes curator lock <skill>, the agent can't change that skill with skill_manage, file edits or terminal writes, even under --yolo, and the curator won't archive it. (#147557 — @daviseford)
  • Leaner built-in skills — a fresh install ships 44 built-in skills instead of 58. Word, PowerPoint and Excel merge into one microsoft-office skill, 12 niche skills become optional installs, and optional skills that vendor repos, catalog MCPs or the built-in web search now cover are removed. The vendor repos for W&B, Qdrant, Pinecone, Notion, Airtable and Shopify are now trusted taps in hermes skills browse. The skills index in the prompt is shorter too. (#146333, #148639, #144174 — @soyluisarca)
  • Skills survive compaction and load by plugin name — every skill that compaction removes, small or slash-loaded, now leaves a reload marker. Plugin skills are named after their plugin (blender:blender) and load even when the model adds plugin: in front. (#138428, #143822, #143784 — @100yenadmin)
  • unbroker handles spam calls — the optional unbroker skill can now start from a phone number and register it on the US Do Not Call list. (#145118 — @SHL0MS)

MCP

  • Tool costs and per-chat spend limits — MCP servers, from plugin packages or config.yaml, can declare what each tool costs and a spend limit per chat. The approval prompt shows the cost and the chat's running total, and a call that would go over the limit asks first, even on a trusted server or in YOLO mode. (#142470, #143787)
  • Clearer approvals for untrusted servers — choosing Allow for this session or Always now actually stops the next prompt for that tool, prompts show the call's arguments with secrets masked, and YOLO or approvals.mode: off skips them. (#143780, #143795, #143787 — @liuhao1024, @fangliquanflq)
  • Live progress for long MCP calls — when a server reports progress, the Desktop tool row and the CLI spinner show the percentage and message, and plugins get a tool.progress event. Servers that declare a longer deadline are no longer cut off by the generic tool timeout. (#141985, #138491)
  • MCP events over webhooks (experimental, opt-in) — a server with mcp_servers.<name>.events: true can notify Hermes when something happens upstream, and each event starts an agent run through the gateway's webhook listener. (#140121 — @Potrock)
  • Connections — servers now see hermes-agent/<version> as the client name, results and tool lists over 1 MiB work on Streamable HTTP, and Otter.ai OAuth logins complete. The MCP catalog adds RentAHuman. (#106395, #143803, #148635, #142246 — @yulunz, @khanaleem727-eng, @liuhao1024, @AlexanderLiteplo)

The Plugin Catalog

🐛 Notable Bug Fixes

  • Background completions on Discord and Telegram reach the agent instead of requeueing forever. (#139026 — @KoNit-K, @sharpnotebot)
  • /retry from messaging works again on multi-profile gateways. (#139149 — @drzxk)
  • A gateway whose pid was recycled after SIGKILL no longer exits 75 forever. (#139855 — @JoaoMarcos44)
  • Messages queued behind a context-exhausted turn now run after the reset. (#139783 — @helix4u)
  • Messages to a chat whose working folder is gone now say so instead of "model request failed". (#143075)
  • Long Claude DirectSDK answers are no longer cancelled by the stall timer. (#142905 — @rborkow)
  • Spilled tool results stay readable for the whole session. (#139894)
  • A Discord /model switch is no longer overwritten as "expired". (#146752 — @oddtazz)
  • Edge TTS works again after Microsoft's token change. (#140896 — @ebrearley)
  • Memory entries stay removable after the character limit is lowered. (#144240)
  • macOS installs can install git again when /usr/bin/git is only the Command Line Tools stub. (#138871 — @unsupportedpastels)
  • The Windows installer no longer gets stuck on a half-installed Git ("INSTALL DIDN'T FINISH"). (#145592 — @xxxigm)
  • Windows Desktop attaches to a Scheduled-Task gateway again. (#139167 — @srymak1r7, @Wenfengcheng)
  • Sandboxed or review Hermes homes no longer rewrite the Windows User PATH or the real hermes.exe. (#137951, #145140 — @jquesnelle)
  • Desktop no longer hangs on macOS when the home path's letter case differs. (#139014 — @dokterdok)
  • Linux Desktop windows are no longer permanently see-through, and renderers no longer crash on /dev/shm. (#144910, #144950 — @cristianob)
  • Linux Desktop restarts come back and keep sudo working. (#145015)
  • Desktop and the Webapp show a Reload notice instead of a blank page when boot fails. (#146092 — @dokterdok)
  • The dashboard can re-pair WhatsApp after a logout. (#142439 — @helix4u)
  • Ctrl+D mid-turn in the classic CLI detaches instead of interrupting the turn. (#140489)
  • Stdio MCP servers on a multi-profile gateway use their own profile's home, not the default (#148129 — @bluemogul1914)
  • Plugin-rewritten messages no longer refused on multi-profile gateways (#139076)
  • Plugin MCP ${VAR} header auth survives reconnects; unset optional vars aren't passed literally (#148879, #141379)
  • hermes plugins doctor no longer copies a plugin into itself until the disk fills (#146836, #146761 — @gyeyeop, @xxxigm)
  • Windows: Desktop plugin names can't escape the plugins folder (#146394)
  • Host-isolated plugins keep provider types, so secret sources apply (#144430)
  • Enabling new deps on a running gateway says restart instead of claiming a reload (#143404 — @benbarclay)
  • Desktop/Webapp install the backend half of manifest-only plugins (#146001 — @m0nty81)
  • ClawHub skills show as installed after install (#145210 — @austinpickett)
  • No more health-check warnings for closed local MCP apps (#148395)

roboflow/supervision ↗ 1 release · 1 stable
0.31.5supervision-0.31.5 Stable2026-11-05T18:35:25Z

v0.31.5: Line counts through occlusion, the exact clipper for oriented boxes, and right-to-left labels

Right-to-left labels, line counting that survives occlusion, and a batch of edge-case fixes across annotators, exports and the NumPy fallback.

  • LineZone counts objects that are hidden for a few frames while they cross the line.
  • Arabic and Hebrew labels render correctly with the new rtl extra.
  • Oriented-box IoU, NMS and metrics now use the exact clipper on OpenCV installs.
  • Annotating a Pillow image keeps its transparency and untouched pixels.
  • Heat maps and ellipse halos now work on grayscale scenes.

It's a drop-in upgrade, but check the Migration guide if you count line crossings, score-oriented boxes on OpenCV, or compare stored outputs.

✨ Spotlights / highlights

Right-to-left labels with the new rtl extra

sv.RichLabelAnnotator now draws Arabic and Hebrew labels correctly on RGB and RGBA images when the new rtl extra is installed. Pillow only joins Arabic letters and orders right-to-left text when it's built with Raqm, and most Windows installs and many containers lack it. There, "سيارة 0.92" came out as isolated letters in reverse order. Lines with Arabic or Hebrew are now shaped in the label's font, using fallback Arabic and Hebrew fonts from khatt for missing glyphs, and right-aligned in the background box. Other image modes fall back to Pillow, with a warning that suggests RGB or RGBA when Raqm is missing. Without the extra, a one-time warning names it. (#2864)

# pip install "supervision[rtl]"
import supervision as sv

label_annotator = sv.RichLabelAnnotator(font_path="NotoNaskhArabic-Regular.ttf")
annotated = label_annotator.annotate(
    scene=image, detections=detections, labels=["سيارة 0.92"]
)

LineZone counts objects occluded while crossing

If a tracker disappears for a few frames while crossing the line, it's still counted. By default, sv.LineZone drops a tracker's stored side after max(30, minimum_crossing_threshold + 1) consecutive absent frames. It used to drop it after max(2, minimum_crossing_threshold + 1), which is 2 at the default threshold. The new max_absent_frames parameter sets this. Counts can rise against 0.31.4 on the same input. (#2873)

import numpy as np
import supervision as sv

line = sv.LineZone(start=sv.Point(0, 50), end=sv.Point(100, 50))


def detection(y):
    return sv.Detections(
        xyxy=np.array([[40.0, y - 10, 60.0, y]]), tracker_id=np.array([1])
    )


line.trigger(detection(40))  # above the line
for _ in range(5):  # occluded for five frames
    line.trigger(sv.Detections.empty())
line.trigger(detection(80))  # reappears below the line
line.in_count, line.out_count
# (0, 1)  (0.31.4: (0, 0))

# Restore the old retention:
sv.LineZone(start=sv.Point(0, 50), end=sv.Point(100, 50), max_absent_frames=2)

The exact clipper for oriented boxes on OpenCV installs

sv.oriented_box_iou_batch, OBB NMS and OBB metrics now use the same convex clipper on OpenCV installs that the NumPy fallback uses. With OpenCV, rotated boxes with nearly collinear overlapping edges, and boxes with both sides far below one coordinate unit, could get an IoU of 0 or 1. The clipper is roughly 10x slower per candidate pair than the OpenCV call, so crowded scenes can take seconds. Oriented-box precision, recall, F1, and mean average recall also keep corner precision at large coordinate origins. (#2882, #2887)

import numpy as np
import supervision as sv


def rotated(width, height, degrees, center=(200.0, 200.0)):
    t = np.deg2rad(degrees)
    u = np.array([np.cos(t), np.sin(t)]) * width / 2
    v = np.array([-np.sin(t), np.cos(t)]) * height / 2
    c = np.array(center)
    return np.array([[c - u - v, c + u - v, c + u + v, c - u + v]])


t = np.deg2rad(10)
a = rotated(100, 40, 10)
b = a + np.array([np.cos(t), np.sin(t)]) * 50  # true IoU is 0.3333
sv.oriented_box_iou_batch(a, b)[0, 0]
# 0.3333  (0.31.4 with OpenCV: 1.0)

Pillow scenes keep the pixels annotators do not draw

Annotators handed a PIL.Image now change only the pixels they draw. A transparent RGBA or LA background stays transparent, and a 16-bit (I;16) or 32-bit (I) image keeps the depth of its untouched pixels, with drawn pixels scaled back to the 16-bit range. Before, the whole image went through an 8-bit round trip. (#2883)

import numpy as np
import supervision as sv
from PIL import Image

scene = Image.new("RGBA", (100, 100), (0, 0, 0, 0))
detections = sv.Detections(
    xyxy=np.array([[20.0, 20.0, 60.0, 60.0]]), class_id=np.array([0])
)
annotated = sv.BoxAnnotator().annotate(scene, detections)
annotated.getpixel((0, 0))
# (0, 0, 0, 0)  (0.31.4: (0, 0, 0, 255))

🔄 Migration guide

No public symbol was removed or renamed, and nothing is deprecated. A few fixes change what existing code returns or draws:

  • Different counts: LineZone keeps a tracker's side for max(30, minimum_crossing_threshold + 1) absent frames instead of 2, so in_count and out_count can rise on the same input. Pass max_absent_frames=max(2, minimum_crossing_threshold + 1) to restore the old retention. (#2873)
  • Different scores and speed on OpenCV installs: oriented-box IoU, NMS and metrics use the exact clipper, roughly 10x slower per candidate pair. Regenerate any stored IoU, NMS and mAP references. (#2882, #2887)
  • Different pixels and files: Pillow scenes keep unannotated pixels (#2883), as_coco writes mask holes joined to their outline (#2879), and the GHUM skeleton gains its left wrist-to-left-index edge (#2876).
  • New error without OpenCV: the cvtColor fallback reads float32 COLOR_HSV2BGR input as OpenCV does (hue in degrees, other values in [0, 1]) and raises ValueError for depths OpenCV rejects. Convert float32 HSV to that scale, or cast to uint8, first. (#2885)
  • Video reads: get_video_frames_generator stops at the requested end instead of reading excluded frames. (#2874)

📝 Notable changes

🚀 Added

  • rtl extra. pip install "supervision[rtl]" installs khatt, and RichLabelAnnotator then shapes Arabic and Hebrew lines in the label font, with fallback fonts from khatt for missing glyphs. Without the extra and without Raqm, a one-time warning names it. (#2864)
  • LineZone(max_absent_frames=...). Sets how many consecutive missing frames pass before a tracker's crossing state is dropped; values below 1 raise ValueError. (#2873)

🌱 Changed

  • LineZone default retention goes from max(2, minimum_crossing_threshold + 1) to max(30, minimum_crossing_threshold + 1) absent frames, so occluded objects are counted. (#2873)
  • oriented_box_iou_batch, OBB NMS and OBB metrics use the convex clipper of the NumPy fallback on OpenCV installs, so IoU can differ for affected pairs; expect roughly 10x the cost per candidate pair. (#2882)

🔧 Fixed

Annotators

  • Annotators given a PIL.Image change only the pixels they draw: RGBA and LA transparency stays, and I;16 and I images keep the depth of untouched pixels. (#2883)
  • HeatMapAnnotator and VertexEllipseHaloAnnotator annotate grayscale scenes, shaped (H, W), (H, W, 1) or a Pillow "L" image, instead of raising ValueError. (#2872, #2878)

Detections, zones and metrics

  • oriented_box_iou_batch on OpenCV installs no longer reports an IoU of 0 or 1 for rotated boxes with nearly collinear overlapping edges, or for boxes with both sides far below one coordinate unit. (#2882)
  • Oriented-box precision, recall, F1 and mean average recall keep corner precision at large coordinate origins, so perfect predictions there are no longer misses or filed under the wrong object-size category. (#2887)
  • np.sum works on a CompactMask, with or without axis, and matches the dense mask for dtype, out, keepdims, initial and where; it raised TypeError before. (#2880)
  • Detections.get_anchors_coordinates(Position.CENTER_OF_MASS) returns an empty (0, 2) array for an empty batch without masks instead of raising ValueError; a populated batch without masks still raises. (#2877)
  • CSVSink keeps an exception raised inside a with block when cleanup also raises; the cleanup exception is logged. (#2869)

Datasets and exports

  • as_coco keeps the holes of masks written as polygons with iscrowd=0, joining them to the outline instead of writing extra polygon parts that readers fill in; a hole that cannot be joined warns and keeps the old parts. (#2879)
  • Hole bridging in as_yolo, as_pascal_voc and as_labelme retries top-down, so valid masks with an enclosed hole listed before its outer hole no longer raise ValueError. (#2879)
  • COCO export computes box width, height and area with Python scalars: without a mask or stored area, a 300×300 float16 or int16 box exports an area of 90000 instead of infinity or 24464. (#2867)

Key points, video and windows

  • Skeleton.GHUM (in supervision.key_points.skeletons) connects the left wrist to the left index finger; EdgeAnnotator had drawn a bone from the right elbow to the right pinky on 33-keypoint poses. (#2876)
  • get_video_frames_generator stops reading and skipping at the requested end. (#2874)
  • ImageWindow.show draws the frame on the call that opens the window, opens at the frame's size up to the screen size without growing on each redraw, and fits frames with a one-pixel dimension. (#2881)

NumPy fallback without OpenCV

  • cvtColor accepts BGRA input for COLOR_BGR2GRAY and (H, W, 1) input for COLOR_GRAY2BGR, so grayscale_image no longer fails on BGRA frames. (#2866)
  • blur on uint16 and int16 images uses exact integer window sums and OpenCV's scaling precision. (#2875)
  • cvtColor reads float32 COLOR_HSV2BGR input as OpenCV does. (#2885)

🏆 Contributors

Welcome to our first-time contributors, Mohammad Essam, Barsam Ershadi, and oha!

  • Barsam Ershadi (@BarsamErshadi, LinkedIn) – grayscale heat maps and halos, COCO mask holes, np.sum on CompactMask, empty-batch center-of-mass anchors, window fixes, the GHUM wrist edge and the oriented-box overlap clipper.
  • kevin (@kevin9327) – made the cvtColor and blur fallbacks follow OpenCV for BGRA, gray, 16-bit and float32 input.
  • Mohammad Essam (@DrEssam97, LinkedIn) – shaped Arabic and Hebrew labels in RichLabelAnnotator with the rtl extra.
  • NIKHIL (@Nikhi00718, LinkedIn) – made LineZone count objects occluded while they cross.
  • A Aswanth Raj (@aswanth-07, LinkedIn) – kept oriented-box metric precision at large coordinate origins.
  • LEE, WEI-HONG (@AuricTW) – fixed COCO area overflow and kept with block exceptions in CSVSink.
  • Mohammad Hijjawi (@MohammadHijjawi97, LinkedIn) – kept unannotated pixels of Pillow scenes unchanged.
  • Devulapalli Naga Sri Vaishnavi (@Vaishnavi220506, LinkedIn) – stopped the video frame generator at the requested end.

microsoft/playwright ↗ 1 release · 1 stable
v1.65.0 Stable2026-11-05T18:25:45Z

🥟 Run tests with Bun

The test runner now runs under Bun. Bun transpiles your TypeScript test files itself, so Playwright does not use Babel or its compilation cache. Pass the --bun flag, because without it bunx playwright test runs on Node.js:

bunx --bun playwright test

Our CI runs the test runner tests under Bun 1.4.3 on Linux. Some features work differently under Bun:

  • There is no watch mode. UI mode does not run tests again when files change, and --only-changed does not follow imports.
  • Bun loads modules itself, so --tsconfig and the JSX options in tsconfig.json have no effect, and custom ESM loaders are not supported.
  • Stack traces do not include async frames, and API names do not include the class name: click instead of locator.click. This changes some step locations, step titles and error messages.
  • Console output of a test does not get into the trace and UI mode.

🚨 Reports with only failures

New failures reporter prints failures and successful retries as they occur, followed by a summary, without progress output.

New onlyFailures option of the HTML, JSON and JUnit reporters keeps only tests with unexpected outcomes, flaky tests and interrupted tests:

// playwright.config.ts
import { defineConfig } from '@playwright/test';

export default defineConfig({
  reporter: [
    ['failures'],
    ['html', { onlyFailures: true }],
  ],
});

🎯 Test runner

  • New built-in -exclusive- test lock runs a test only when no other tests are running. All other tests continue to run in parallel with each other:

    test('restart the server', { lock: '-exclusive-' }, async () => {
  // No other test runs while this one is running.
});

  • test.skip(), test.fixme(), test.fail() and test.slow() now accept only a description. It is recorded in the test annotation:

    test('upload a file', async ({ page }) => {
  test.skip('Not implemented yet');
});

  • New PLAYWRIGHT_TRANSFORM_CACHE_DIR environment variable sets the directory for transpiled test files, for example on a Linux machine shared with other users.

New APIs

  • New options timeout and signal in cdpSession.send().
  • New testRun.mode() returns the execution mode of a suite during reporter.preprocess().

Breaking Changes ⚠️

  • TypeScript class fields now follow useDefineForClassFields from your tsconfig.json, the same as tsc. It is enabled for ES2022 and later targets, and when target is not specified. A field initializer that reads a constructor parameter property now fails, because the initializer runs first:

    class LoginPage {
  // TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'getByRole')
  readonly submit = this.page.getByRole('button', { name: 'Submit' });

  constructor(private readonly page: Page) {}
}

    Move the initialization to the constructor, or set "useDefineForClassFields": false in your tsconfig.json.

  • Actions such as locator.click(), locator.check(), locator.tap(), locator.fill(), locator.clear() and locator.selectOption() now wait for the element to be not inert. An action on an element with the inert attribute, or inside such an element, now times out if the element stays inert.

  • A sharded run now fails with "No tests found" when no tests match across all shards. Pass --pass-with-no-tests to allow this. An empty shard still passes when other shards have tests.

  • Cookie matching now follows RFC 6265. With a URL, browserContext.cookies() and apiRequestContext.cookies() no longer return host-only cookies for subdomains or Path=/api cookies for /apiv2, and a Path=/foo/ cookie does not match /foo. APIRequestContext sends a cookie without a name as Cookie: x instead of Cookie: =x, and stores Set-Cookie: token as a cookie without a name.

  • Proxy credentials are no longer sent to websites. The credentials in the proxy option now answer only proxy challenges. To authenticate on a website, use httpCredentials.

  • Text that the browser does not render, such as text that is not assigned to a slot or text directly inside a closed <details>, is no longer part of accessible names and aria snapshots. This can change the results of page.getByRole() with a name, locator.ariaSnapshot() and expect(locator).toMatchAriaSnapshot().

  • String and URLSearchParams values of the params option in APIRequestContext requests are now appended to the query that the URL already has, instead of replacing it.

  • .css, .less and .scss imports in test files now load as empty modules instead of being removed, so the imported file must exist.

  • The blob reporter again deletes the content of its output directory before it writes the report. Playwright 1.64 kept reports with other names.

  • Test locations of imported calls such as test( in CommonJS output now point to the start of the callee, for example a.spec.ts:3:7 instead of a.spec.ts:3:11. This is the same as in ESM.

Announcements

  • ⚠️ Playwright no longer tests on Node.js 20. Use Node.js 22, 24 or 26, as listed in the system requirements.
  • 🐳 Docker images now include Node.js 26 instead of Node.js 24, and the latest patch of Chromium on the same branch.

Browser Versions

  • Chromium 158.0.8127.18
  • Mozilla Firefox 159.0
  • WebKit 27.2

This version was also tested against the following stable channels:

  • Google Chrome 157
  • Microsoft Edge 156

View release →

Graphify-Labs/graphify ↗ 1 release · 1 stable
v0.9.103graphify 0.9.103 Stable2026-11-05T16:35:13Z

graphify 0.9.103

A broad correctness, performance and robustness release across the extractors, resolution, hooks, the query and update paths, install and the skill runbook.

Extractors

  • C#: preprocessor directives and #if branches that derail tree-sitter are blanked before parsing (byte-length preserving), so a directive no longer closes a class early and drops later members or calls.
  • Solidity: library calls (Lib.f(), including aliased imports) and emit/revert/require naming a declared event or error now link to the member they name, gated to a unique visible type and member.
  • YAML: a << merge sequence gives the earlier anchor precedence for a duplicated key, matching the spec and PyYAML.
  • Markdown: a code span written as a backslash namespace (\Foo\Bar) or a type-qualified member (Type::member) resolves to the real node; a dotted mention whose method name is a language builtin is no longer dropped.
  • Python: a bare builtin-named call (raise NotImplementedError(...)) no longer binds to a same-named class in another module unless imported.

Resolution

  • A call inside a top-level def _main() is attributed to _main, not the public main it collides with after id normalization, without misattributing a class member's type reference.

Hooks

  • A file unchanged in content is not re-flagged stale by mtime (the extraction hash is checked against the manifest).
  • A dedicated Grep is held to the strict orientation gate; a Bash file read (cat/head/sed -n) of an in-project code file gets the read nudge; a search whose every target is outside the project emits no project hint; the nudge budget is restored after a Claude Code compaction; and the toplevel anchor keeps working when CDPATH is exported.

Performance

  • Lower peak memory in load, resolution and extraction; a faster #3252 cross-file repoint; and the watch reconcile pass resolves AST ownership once per file instead of re-reading each header per node — all with identical graph output.

Update / build / cache

  • graphify update re-extracts only the files whose content changed (identical to a full rebuild); a contended incremental request is recorded instead of dropped. The build write guard no longer counts intentional ghost/dedup folds as drops; cache keys keep their written case.

Query / tree

  • Source nodes rank above tests and docs (a soft weight) with file-first answers; query reads query_budget/query_depth from .graphifyrc and gains --depth. export tree caps directory children while reporting full totals.

Install / thread-safety

  • The Pi project skill is written to .pi/skills; uninstall removes the global skill registration it wrote. The ambient scan root is held in a ContextVar so concurrent library callers extracting different roots do not clobber each other.

The AST cache schema is bumped to 39; warm caches re-run the affected extractions.

Thanks to @rohit-jsfreaky, @Deepjyoti-Sarmah, @xiongjianxu, @nothariharan, @andrebrait, @ajbmachon, @Shubham-Padkonde, @madhusudanpatnaik, @pei711, @Mpasha17, @ayushcodes10, @gitmotion, @briascoi, @oleksii-tumanov, @rohit-jsfreaky, @viwe-monai, @WyattAu, @Vivek1898, @wanjinhao1, @PratikDhanave, @HhH666-ux, @sunhouy, @mathewgeejo, @jinsoo and @AndresDiagoM.

View release →
